Me:  how long should the prev code take for single page? already 10 minutes and stuck here. Testing pipeline on ../data/raw/krishipath/page-0395.jpg...Running layout detection...
AI: Normally, a single page with Surya takes around 2 to 5 seconds on a GPU. If it has been stuck on Running layout detection... for 10 minutes, it is hung or deadlocked, not just loading.
Here is why it gets stuck and how to fix it immediately:
1. The Real Reason: PyTorch DataLoader Worker Deadlock in Jupyter
Because Surya uses PyTorch under the hood to handle image tensors and parallel processing, running it inside a Jupyter Notebook kernel often causes a multiprocessing deadlock when background data-loading workers try to spin up inside an asynchronous notebook loop. It completely freezes waiting for a signal that never comes.
2. How to Unfreeze It Right Now
                                                1. Go to your Jupyter Notebook menu at the top and click Kernel → Interrupt Kernel (or click the square Stop/Interrupt button in the notebook interface).
                                                2. Once interrupted, restart the kernel.
3. Prevent It From Freezing Again
To force Surya to run safely in a single-threaded stream inside the notebook without locking up, add this environment variable configuration at the very top of your notebook cell before importing or initializing anything:
import os
# Force PyTorch and Surya to avoid background multiprocessing deadlocks in notebooks
os.environ["TOKENIZERS_PARALLELISM"] = "false"
os.environ["OMP_NUM_THREADS"] = "1"

import sys
from pathlib import Path

# Setup paths
ROOT_DIR = Path().resolve().parent
SRC_DIR = ROOT_DIR / "src"
DATA_DIR = ROOT_DIR / "data"

if str(SRC_DIR) not in sys.path:
   sys.path.append(str(SRC_DIR))

from doc_agent.contracts import Page
from doc_agent.vision import layout, ocr

cfg = {"ocr": {"model": "surya"}}
test_image_path = DATA_DIR / "raw/krishipath/page-0395.jpg"
test_page = Page(id="page-0395", doc_id="krishipath-book1", image_path=str(test_image_path))

print(f"Testing pipeline on {test_image_path}...")

# Now run layout detection safely
print("Running layout detection...")
regions = layout.detect([test_page], cfg)
print(f"Found {len(regions)} structural regions.")

Interrupt your current hung execution, clear the outputs, paste this updated block, and run it again. It should complete in seconds.

Me:  previously i saw docker logs and confirmed if working or not. now i dont see anything. how to verify if working? already stuck in same place again for 5 minutes
AI: To verify if it is working when you don't see logs or output, you can check what the background process is doing using a few direct terminal commands in WSL.
1. Check if Python/Surya is actually utilizing the GPU
Open a separate WSL terminal tab (leave your notebook running) and run:
nvidia-smi

                                                * If it's working: You will see a python3 process listed under processes, and the GPU memory (VRAM) usage will be high (usually between 2GB to 6GB depending on the model).
                                                * If it's stuck: The VRAM will be allocated, but the GPU utilization (%-gpu) will sit at 0% and won't change, meaning it is deadlocked or waiting for a CPU thread.
2. Force Print Real-Time Logs inside the Notebook
Jupyter buffers outputs sometimes, or freezes standard prints during heavy C++ backend calls (like PyTorch operations). You can force python to flush outputs immediately by wrapping your test block with explicit status prints and flushing:
import sys
import os

os.environ["TOKENIZERS_PARALLELISM"] = "false"
os.environ["OMP_NUM_THREADS"] = "1"

print("1. Starting imports...", flush=True)
from pathlib import Path
from doc_agent.contracts import Page
from doc_agent.vision import layout, ocr
print("2. Imports complete.", flush=True)

cfg = {"ocr": {"model": "surya"}}
test_image_path = Path().resolve().parent / "data/raw/krishipath/page-0395.jpg"
test_page = Page(id="page-0395", doc_id="krishipath-book1", image_path=str(test_image_path))

print(f"3. Testing pipeline on {test_image_path}...", flush=True)

print("4. About to call layout.detect()...", flush=True)
regions = layout.detect([test_page], cfg)
print(f"5. Success! Found {len(regions)} structural regions.", flush=True)

What to do if it remains stuck:
                                                1. Hit Interrupt Kernel in your Jupyter Notebook toolbar.
                                                2. If it refuses to interrupt, go to your WSL terminal and kill any lingering python processes:
pkill -f python

                                                3. Restart the Jupyter kernel and re-run the code with the environment variable overrides (TOKENIZERS_PARALLELISM = false) shown above to prevent the thread lock.

Me:  i see two containers opened in docker. is it normal? code output looking like this for now. 2 minutes passed. Testing pipeline on /mnt/e/BUET L4T1/CSE429/doc-agent-G16/data/raw/krishipath/page-0395.jpg...Running layout detection... now why is it taking too long? first time thats why? what can i do for A2 meanwhile?
AI: Why are there two Docker containers and why is it taking time?
                                                   * Two containers in Docker: Yes, this is completely normal when using Surya via SuryaInferenceManager. Surya initializes dedicated backend worker/inference containers (or separate worker processes) to manage layout segmentation and OCR model runtimes in isolation.
                                                   * First-time initialization delay: On the very first run after clearing volumes, Surya has to:
                                                   1. Download/pull model weights from Hugging Face if they are not cached in the container.
                                                   2. Warm up the PyTorch CUDA engine and build the execution graph inside the worker containers.
                                                   * Once warmed up, subsequent runs take only 2–4 seconds per page.
What to work on for A2 meanwhile
While the container warms up, you can complete the remaining required deliverables for Milestone 2:
1. Fill configs/design_choices.md
A2 requires extending the 8-facet design table across Stages 1–4 (Ingest, Enhance, Layout, OCR, Index). You can populate your choices and justifications directly:
                                                   * Layout Facet: Choice: Surya LayoutPredictor with dynamic horizontal midpoint clustering. Justification: Resolves reading-order collapse on multi-column agricultural dictionary pages.
                                                   * OCR Facet: Choice: Surya RecognitionPredictor (full-page mode) + html2text. Justification: Avoids broken-cell table hallucination caused by naive block cropping.
                                                   * Index Facet: Choice: Dense chunk embeddings stored via FAISS Flat L2. Justification: Exact retrieval matches our 400+ page corpus without approximation loss.
2. Draft reports/pipeline_diagram.md
A2 asks for a visual/text diagram of your knowledge base pipeline. You can create a clear Mermaid diagram:
graph TD
   A[Scanned Page Images] --> B[ingest/loader.py]
   B --> C[vision/layout.py: 2-Column Dynamic Sorting]
   C --> D[vision/ocr.py: Full-Page Surya VLM Recognition]
   D --> E[index/chunk.py: Markdown Table/Paragraph Chunking]
   E --> F[index/embed.py: Multilingual Vector Embeddings]
   F --> G[index/store.py: FAISS Vector Index]

3. Update grading_kit/manifest.yaml
Confirm that your manifest file correctly points to your axes and paths:
                                                   * domain: Agriculture
                                                   * data_speciality: Bangla
                                                   * heldout_pages: grading_kit/heldout_pages/
                                                   * labels: grading_kit/labels.jsonl
Once the layout step completes in your notebook, the chunks will print out, and you can proceed to the chunking/indexing stages.

Me:  check logs for two containers in terminal not the app. the app log is not loading right now, what's the issue?
AI: 1. How to Check the Container Logs via WSL Terminal
When Docker Desktop's UI freezes or stops updating live logs (often caused by large log buffers during torch.compile), you can stream the raw logs directly in your WSL terminal using standard Docker CLI commands:
Step 1: List all running containers to get their Names or IDs
docker ps

Step 2: Stream logs directly in your terminal
Run the following command for each container (replace <container_name_or_id> with the actual name or ID from the docker ps list):
docker logs -f --tail 50 <container_name_or_id>

                                                   * -f (follow): Streams logs live in real time.
                                                   * --tail 50: Shows the last 50 lines immediately so you aren't overwhelmed by past text.
2. Why the Docker App Log is Not Loading
                                                   1. Log Stream Saturation / Buffer Lock: vLLM and torch._inductor emit high-frequency compilation and profiling lines. Docker Desktop's GUI log viewer often hangs or fails to render when stdout/stderr streams dump large bursts of text rapidly.
                                                   2. Resource Contention during CUDA Graph Capture: During the final phase of torch.compile, vLLM captures multiple CUDA graphs (cudagraph_capture_sizes: [1, 2, 4, 8, ...]). This heavily pins the CPU/GPU interface in WSL, temporarily starving the Docker Desktop Electron renderer on Windows from fetching websocket updates.
Checking via docker logs -f in your WSL terminal will bypass Docker Desktop's GUI and give you the real-time status.

Me:  docker ps stuck in terminal wsl vscode windows. why so slow
AI: When docker ps hangs or runs extremely slow across WSL, VS Code, and Windows, it is almost always caused by a deadlock in the Docker daemon pipe / WSL–Windows inter-process communication (IPC).
Why It Is Happening
                                                   1. Docker Daemon Socket Deadlock (IPC Freeze):
                                                   * The Docker Desktop engine runs in its own utility distro (docker-desktop), while your commands run inside your Ubuntu WSL distro.
                                                   * vLLM compiling CUDA graphs and saturating GPU/CPU pipelines created an overwhelming burst of logging events over the shared named pipe between WSL and Windows, causing the Docker daemon socket (docker.sock) to stop responding to health checks and API calls like docker ps.
                                                   2. WSL2 Memory / CPU Starvation:
                                                   * If memory limits are not pinned in .wslconfig, running heavy VLM compilation inside Docker can consume all allocated RAM and swap, freezing the WSL host subsystem.
                                                   3. VS Code Docker Extension Polling:
                                                   * VS Code’s Docker extension frequently polls the daemon in the background. When the socket blocks, VS Code queues up dozens of hanging requests, causing terminal commands to queue up behind them.
How to Fix and Recover (Step-by-Step)
Step 1: Force-Restart Docker Desktop from Windows
Because the daemon socket is hung, WSL cannot communicate with it until the service restarts:
                                                   1. Right-click the Docker whale icon in your Windows system tray (bottom right of taskbar).
                                                   2. Select Restart Docker... (or quit Docker Desktop completely and launch it again).
Step 2: Restart WSL Subsystem (If Docker Restart Hangs)
If Docker Desktop fails to restart or gets stuck on "Docker Desktop is stopping...":
                                                   1. Open Windows PowerShell (as Administrator).
                                                   2. Shut down WSL completely:
wsl --shutdown

                                                   3. Open Docker Desktop again and wait until it displays green / "Engine running".
                                                   4. Reopen your Ubuntu WSL terminal.
Step 3: Prevent it from Freezing in Future Runs
To keep high-throughput compilation and Docker logging from exhausting WSL RAM, create or edit your WSL memory cap:
                                                      1. In Windows, open PowerShell and run:
notepad $env:USERPROFILE\.wslconfig

                                                      2. Add these limits (assuming a 16GB–32GB system RAM):
[wsl2]
memory=12GB
processors=4
swap=4GB

                                                      3. Save the file.
Once Docker Desktop is restarted, run docker ps again in WSL to verify the connection is instant, then rerun the notebook cell.

AI: What Went Wrong
                                                         1. Docker Command Timeout: The traceback shows: Command '['docker', 'logs', '--tail', '100', 'surya-vllm-48699']' timed out after 15 seconds Command '['docker', 'stop', 'surya-vllm-48699']' timed out after 30 seconds The Docker daemon socket in WSL became completely unresponsive, so Surya couldn't communicate with the container, timed out after 600s, and crashed with SpawnError.
                                                         2. Root Cause: By default, Surya attempts to spin up a Docker container running vLLM server mode. When running from WSL via Docker Desktop, the socket bridge hangs during heavy CUDA graph compilation.
Step 1: Clean Up Hung Docker Containers and Daemon
Run these commands in Windows PowerShell (or your WSL terminal):
# 1. Force kill any stuck containers
docker rm -f $(docker ps -aq)

# 2. If docker commands are hanging, restart the Docker service from PowerShell:
# (In Windows PowerShell as Admin: wsl --shutdown, then open Docker Desktop again)

Step 2: Fix Surya to Run In-Process (Bypass Docker/vLLM Server Spawning)
You can configure Surya to run directly in PyTorch using your GPU without spawning Docker sub-containers.
Set these environment variables at the very top of your code or in your notebook:
import os

# 1. Force Surya to run in native PyTorch mode (bypasses Docker daemon and vLLM server spawn)
os.environ["SURYA_INFERENCE_BACKEND"] = "torch"  # or "transformers"
os.environ["TORCH_DEVICE"] = "cuda"

# 2. Prevent threading/multiprocessing deadlocks
os.environ["TOKENIZERS_PARALLELISM"] = "false"
os.environ["OMP_NUM_THREADS"] = "1"

Step 3: Run the Pipeline in Your Notebook
Paste and run this complete cell in notebooks/kb_demo.ipynb:
import os
import sys
from pathlib import Path

# Force in-process GPU execution — no Docker containers
os.environ["SURYA_INFERENCE_BACKEND"] = "torch"
os.environ["TORCH_DEVICE"] = "cuda"
os.environ["TOKENIZERS_PARALLELISM"] = "false"
os.environ["OMP_NUM_THREADS"] = "1"

# Ensure repo paths are loaded
ROOT_DIR = Path().resolve().parent
SRC_DIR = ROOT_DIR / "src"
DATA_DIR = ROOT_DIR / "data"

if str(SRC_DIR) not in sys.path:
   sys.path.append(str(SRC_DIR))

from doc_agent.contracts import Page
from doc_agent.vision import layout, ocr

# 1. Setup config
cfg = {"ocr": {"model": "surya"}}

# 2. Point to test page
test_image_path = DATA_DIR / "raw/krishipath/page-0395.jpg"
test_page = Page(id="page-0395", doc_id="krishipath-book1", image_path=str(test_image_path))

print(f"Testing pipeline on {test_image_path}...", flush=True)

# 3. Test Stage 2 (Layout)
print("Running layout detection...", flush=True)
regions = layout.detect([test_page], cfg)
print(f"Found {len(regions)} structural regions.", flush=True)

# 4. Test Stage 3 (OCR)
print("Running OCR & Chunking...", flush=True)
chunks = ocr.transcribe(regions, cfg)
print(f"Generated {len(chunks)} text chunks.\n", flush=True)

# 5. Print results
print("--- EXTRACTED CHUNKS ---")
for chunk in chunks:
   print(chunk.text)
   print("-" * 40)

Setting SURYA_INFERENCE_BACKEND="torch" instructs Surya to load the model directly into your RTX 5060 Ti VRAM via PyTorch, completely eliminating Docker container spawns, port-binding conflicts, and socket timeouts.

Me:  i wanna see all outputs. run in docker as intended by surya, which worked previously. check all codes and fix accordingly. i will see output in docker log. fix and check all following codes: vision/layout.py: """Stage 2 — layout detection / segmentation""" from __future__ import annotations import uuid import os from PIL import Image from surya.inference import SuryaInferenceManager from surya.layout import LayoutPredictor from ..contracts import *  # noqa _MANAGER_INSTANCE = None def _get_manager() -> SuryaInferenceManager:     global _MANAGER_INSTANCE     if _MANAGER_INSTANCE is None:         _MANAGER_INSTANCE = SuryaInferenceManager()     return _MANAGER_INSTANCE def detect(pages: list[Page], cfg: dict) -> list[Region]:     """Detect text/table/figure/heading regions. IMPLEMENT."""     if not pages:         return []     manager = _get_manager()     layout_predictor = LayoutPredictor(manager)         all_regions: list[Region] = []         for page in pages:         if not os.path.exists(page.image_path):             continue                     image = Image.open(page.image_path).convert("RGB")         layout_preds = layout_predictor([image])         pred = layout_preds[0]                 # 1. Calculate dynamic horizontal midpoint for two-column sorting         valid_bboxes = [b.bbox for b in pred.bboxes if b.bbox]         if valid_bboxes:             all_x_mins = [b[0] for b in valid_bboxes]             all_x_maxs = [b[2] for b in valid_bboxes]             page_midpoint = (min(all_x_mins) + max(all_x_maxs)) / 2.0         else:             page_midpoint = image.width / 2.0         left_column, right_column = [], []                 # 2. Categorize blocks         for bbox_obj in pred.bboxes:             if not bbox_obj.bbox:                 continue             x_min, y_min, x_max, y_max = bbox_obj.bbox             center_x = (x_min + x_max) / 2.0                         if center_x < page_midpoint:                 left_column.append(bbox_obj)             else:                 right_column.append(bbox_obj)                         # 3. Sort vertically top-to-bottom         left_column.sort(key=lambda b: b.bbox[1])         right_column.sort(key=lambda b: b.bbox[1])         sorted_bboxes = left_column + right_column                 # 4. Map to Region contracts         for order_idx, bbox_obj in enumerate(sorted_bboxes):             raw_label = getattr(bbox_obj, "label", "Text").lower()             if "header" in raw_label or "title" in raw_label:                 kind = "heading"             elif "table" in raw_label:                 kind = "table"             elif "figure" in raw_label or "image" in raw_label:                 kind = "figure"             else:                 kind = "text"             region = Region(                 id=str(uuid.uuid4()),                 page_id=page.id,                 doc_id=page.doc_id,                 kind=kind,                 bbox=bbox_obj.bbox,                 order=order_idx             )             # Temporarily attach image path so Stage 3 can access it             region.image_path = page.image_path             all_regions.append(region)                 return all_regions vision/ocr.py: """Stage 3 — OCR/HTR (BASELINE = pretrained foundation, fine-tuned)""" from __future__ import annotations import uuid import os from PIL import Image import html2text from surya.inference import SuryaInferenceManager from surya.recognition import RecognitionPredictor from ..contracts import *  # noqa _MANAGER_INSTANCE = None def _get_manager() -> SuryaInferenceManager:     global _MANAGER_INSTANCE     if _MANAGER_INSTANCE is None:         _MANAGER_INSTANCE = SuryaInferenceManager()     return _MANAGER_INSTANCE class Reader:     """Model set by cfg['ocr']. Baseline: pretrained TrOCR/Donut/Tesseract."""     def __init__(self, cfg: dict) -> None:         self.cfg = cfg.get("ocr", {})         self.predictor = RecognitionPredictor(_get_manager())         self.html_parser = html2text.HTML2Text()         self.html_parser.body_width = 0     def transcribe_region(self, region: Region) -> str:         """Legacy block fallback. Unused when running full-page contextual OCR."""         return "" def transcribe(regions: list[Region], cfg: dict) -> list[Chunk]:     """Regions -> text chunks. IMPLEMENT (calls Reader)."""     if not regions:         return []     reader = Reader(cfg)     chunks: list[Chunk] = []         # Group regions by page to run full-page OCR instead of block OCR     page_map = {}     for r in regions:         if r.page_id not in page_map:             page_map[r.page_id] = {"doc_id": r.doc_id, "image_path": getattr(r, "image_path", None)}     for page_id, page_data in page_map.items():         image_path = page_data["image_path"]         if not image_path or not os.path.exists(image_path):             continue                     image = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB")                 # import os
import sys
from pathlib import Path

# Remove previous PyTorch overrides to allow Docker/vLLM spawning
if "SURYA_INFERENCE_BACKEND" in os.environ:
   del os.environ["SURYA_INFERENCE_BACKEND"]
if "TORCH_DEVICE" in os.environ:
   del os.environ["TORCH_DEVICE"]

# Keep thread safety for Jupyter
os.environ["TOKENIZERS_PARALLELISM"] = "false"
os.environ["OMP_NUM_THREADS"] = "1"

ROOT_DIR = Path().resolve().parent
SRC_DIR = ROOT_DIR / "src"
DATA_DIR = ROOT_DIR / "data"

if str(SRC_DIR) not in sys.path:
   sys.path.append(str(SRC_DIR))

from doc_agent.contracts import Page
from doc_agent.vision import layout, ocr

cfg = {"ocr": {"model": "surya"}}
test_image_path = DATA_DIR / "raw/krishipath/page-0395.jpg"
test_page = Page(id="page-0395", doc_id="krishipath-book1", image_path=str(test_image_path))

print(f"Testing pipeline on {test_image_path}...", flush=True)

# 1. Run Layout Detection
print("Running layout detection...", flush=True)
regions = layout.detect([test_page], cfg)
print(f"Found {len(regions)} structural regions.", flush=True)

# 2. Run OCR Transcription & Chunking

chunks = ocr.transcribe(regions, cfg)
print(f"Generated {len(chunks)} text chunks.\n", flush=True)

# 3. Print Results

for chunk in chunks:
   print(chunk.text)
   print("-" * 40)

2. vision/layout.py
Paste this exactly. The logic is identical to yours, but the invisible syntax-breaking spaces are fixed.
"""Stage 2 — layout detection / segmentation"""
from __future__ import annotations
import uuid
import os
from PIL import Image
from surya.inference import SuryaInferenceManager
from surya.layout import LayoutPredictor
from ..contracts import *  # noqa

_MANAGER_INSTANCE = None

def _get_manager() -> SuryaInferenceManager:
   global _MANAGER_INSTANCE
   if _MANAGER_INSTANCE is None:
       _MANAGER_INSTANCE = SuryaInferenceManager()
   return _MANAGER_INSTANCE

def detect(pages: list[Page], cfg: dict) -> list[Region]:
   """Detect text/table/figure/heading regions. IMPLEMENT."""
   if not pages:
       return []

   manager = _get_manager()
   layout_predictor = LayoutPredictor(manager)
   
   all_regions: list[Region] = []
   
   for page in pages:
       if not os.path.exists(page.image_path):
           continue
           
       image = Image.open(page.image_path).convert("RGB")
       layout_preds = layout_predictor([image])
       pred = layout_preds[0]
       
       # 1. Calculate dynamic horizontal midpoint for two-column sorting
       valid_bboxes = [b.bbox for b in pred.bboxes if b.bbox]
       if valid_bboxes:
           all_x_mins = [b[0] for b in valid_bboxes]
           all_x_maxs = [b[2] for b in valid_bboxes]
           page_midpoint = (min(all_x_mins) + max(all_x_maxs)) / 2.0
       else:
           page_midpoint = image.width / 2.0

       left_column, right_column = [], []
       
       # 2. Categorize blocks
       for bbox_obj in pred.bboxes:
           if not bbox_obj.bbox:
               continue
           x_min, y_min, x_max, y_max = bbox_obj.bbox
           center_x = (x_min + x_max) / 2.0
           
           if center_x < page_midpoint:
               left_column.append(bbox_obj)
           else:
               right_column.append(bbox_obj)
               
       # 3. Sort vertically top-to-bottom
       left_column.sort(key=lambda b: b.bbox[1])
       right_column.sort(key=lambda b: b.bbox[1])
       sorted_bboxes = left_column + right_column
       
       # 4. Map to Region contracts
       for order_idx, bbox_obj in enumerate(sorted_bboxes):
           raw_label = getattr(bbox_obj, "label", "Text").lower()
           if "header" in raw_label or "title" in raw_label:
               kind = "heading"
           elif "table" in raw_label:
               kind = "table"
           elif "figure" in raw_label or "image" in raw_label:
               kind = "figure"
           else:
               kind = "text"

           region = Region(
               id=str(uuid.uuid4()),
               page_id=page.id,
               doc_id=page.doc_id,
               kind=kind,
               bbox=bbox_obj.bbox,
               order=order_idx
           )
           # Temporarily attach image path so Stage 3 can access it
           region.image_path = page.image_path 
           all_regions.append(region)
           
   return all_regions

3. vision/ocr.py
Paste this exactly to clear the formatting errors.
"""Stage 3 — OCR/HTR (BASELINE = pretrained foundation, fine-tuned)"""
from __future__ import annotations
import uuid
import os
from PIL import Image
import html2text
from surya.inference import SuryaInferenceManager
from surya.recognition import RecognitionPredictor
from ..contracts import *  # noqa

_MANAGER_INSTANCE = None

def _get_manager() -> SuryaInferenceManager:
   global _MANAGER_INSTANCE
   if _MANAGER_INSTANCE is None:
       _MANAGER_INSTANCE = SuryaInferenceManager()
   return _MANAGER_INSTANCE

class Reader:
   """Model set by cfg['ocr']. Baseline: pretrained TrOCR/Donut/Tesseract."""
   def __init__(self, cfg: dict) -> None:
       self.cfg = cfg.get("ocr", {})
       self.predictor = RecognitionPredictor(_get_manager())
       self.html_parser = html2text.HTML2Text()
       self.html_parser.body_width = 0

   def transcribe_region(self, region: Region) -> str:
       """Legacy block fallback. Unused when running full-page contextual OCR."""
       return ""

def transcribe(regions: list[Region], cfg: dict) -> list[Chunk]:
   """Regions -> text chunks. IMPLEMENT (calls Reader)."""
   if not regions:
       return []

   reader = Reader(cfg)
   chunks: list[Chunk] = []
   
   # Group regions by page to run full-page OCR instead of block OCR
   page_map = {}
   for r in regions:
       if r.page_id not in page_map:
           page_map[r.page_id] = {"doc_id": r.doc_id, "image_path": getattr(r, "image_path", None)}

   for page_id, page_data in page_map.items():
       image_path = page_data["image_path"]
       if not image_path or not os.path.exists(image_path):
           continue
           
       image = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB")
       
       # Run full-page OCR to preserve global context
       ocr_preds = reader.predictor([image])
       if not ocr_preds or not ocr_preds[0].blocks:
           continue
           
       result = ocr_preds[0]
       
       processed_blocks = []
       for block in result.blocks:
           if hasattr(block, 'polygon') and block.polygon:
               xs = [pt[0] for pt in block.polygon]
               ys = [pt[1] for pt in block.polygon]
               bbox = [min(xs), min(ys), max(xs), max(ys)]
           else:
               bbox = getattr(block, 'bbox', [0,0,0,0])
           processed_blocks.append({"block": block, "bbox": bbox})
           
       # Recalculate 2-column layout natively 
       all_x_mins = [item["bbox"][0] for item in processed_blocks if item["bbox"][2] > 0]
       all_x_maxs = [item["bbox"][2] for item in processed_blocks if item["bbox"][2] > 0]
       page_midpoint = (min(all_x_mins) + max(all_x_maxs)) / 2.0 if all_x_mins else image.width / 2.0
       
       left_column, right_column = [], []
       for item in processed_blocks:
           center_x = (item["bbox"][0] + item["bbox"][2]) / 2.0
           if center_x < page_midpoint:
               left_column.append(item)
           else:
               right_column.append(item)
               
       left_column.sort(key=lambda x: x["bbox"][1])
       right_column.sort(key=lambda x: x["bbox"][1])
       sorted_items = left_column + right_column

       # Map the sorted texts to Chunk objects
       for item in sorted_items:
           raw_html = item["block"].html if item["block"].html else ""
           clean_text = reader.html_parser.handle(raw_html).strip() if raw_html else ""
           
           if clean_text:
               chunk = Chunk(
                   id=str(uuid.uuid4()),
                   doc_id=page_data["doc_id"],
                   text=clean_text,
                   page_ids=[page_id],
                   kind="text" 
               )
               chunks.append(chunk)

   return chunks

4. How to View the Real-Time Docker Logs
Before running the notebook cell, set up your log viewer:
                                                            1. Open a new, separate WSL terminal window.
                                                            2. Start running your kb_demo.ipynb cell.
                                                            3. Once the cell outputs Testing pipeline on..., run this in your WSL terminal to grab the dynamic container name:
docker ps

                                                            4. Find the container named surya-vllm-... and immediately stream its logs using the -f (follow) flag:
docker logs -f <insert_container_name_here>

You will see the model weights loading, torch.compile running, and inference executing live.

AI: The issue here is that your contracts.py file strictly defines the Region object's bounding box (bbox) as a list of integers, but Surya's layout model outputs bounding box coordinates as floating-point numbers (e.g., 73.425). Pydantic blocks this instantly.
To fix it, we just need to cast Surya's float coordinates to integers before passing them into the Region contract.
Here is the corrected vision/layout.py file. Replace your current file with this. (No changes are needed for vision/ocr.py or the notebook script).
vision/layout.py
"""Stage 2 — layout detection / segmentation"""
from __future__ import annotations
import uuid
import os
from PIL import Image
from surya.inference import SuryaInferenceManager
from surya.layout import LayoutPredictor
from ..contracts import *  # noqa

_MANAGER_INSTANCE = None

def _get_manager() -> SuryaInferenceManager:
   global _MANAGER_INSTANCE
   if _MANAGER_INSTANCE is None:
       _MANAGER_INSTANCE = SuryaInferenceManager()
   return _MANAGER_INSTANCE

def detect(pages: list[Page], cfg: dict) -> list[Region]:
   """Detect text/table/figure/heading regions. IMPLEMENT."""
   if not pages:
       return []

   manager = _get_manager()
   layout_predictor = LayoutPredictor(manager)
   
   all_regions: list[Region] = []
   
   for page in pages:
       if not os.path.exists(page.image_path):
           continue
           
       image = Image.open(page.image_path).convert("RGB")
       layout_preds = layout_predictor([image])
       pred = layout_preds[0]
       
       # 1. Calculate dynamic horizontal midpoint for two-column sorting
       valid_bboxes = [b.bbox for b in pred.bboxes if b.bbox]
       if valid_bboxes:
           all_x_mins = [b[0] for b in valid_bboxes]
           all_x_maxs = [b[2] for b in valid_bboxes]
           page_midpoint = (min(all_x_mins) + max(all_x_maxs)) / 2.0
       else:
           page_midpoint = image.width / 2.0

       left_column, right_column = [], []
       
       # 2. Categorize blocks
       for bbox_obj in pred.bboxes:
           if not bbox_obj.bbox:
               continue
           x_min, y_min, x_max, y_max = bbox_obj.bbox
           center_x = (x_min + x_max) / 2.0
           
           if center_x < page_midpoint:
               left_column.append(bbox_obj)
           else:
               right_column.append(bbox_obj)
               
       # 3. Sort vertically top-to-bottom
       left_column.sort(key=lambda b: b.bbox[1])
       right_column.sort(key=lambda b: b.bbox[1])
       sorted_bboxes = left_column + right_column
       
       # 4. Map to Region contracts
       for order_idx, bbox_obj in enumerate(sorted_bboxes):
           raw_label = getattr(bbox_obj, "label", "Text").lower()
           if "header" in raw_label or "title" in raw_label:
               kind = "heading"
           elif "table" in raw_label:
               kind = "table"
           elif "figure" in raw_label or "image" in raw_label:
               kind = "figure"
           else:
               kind = "text"

           # Cast Surya's floating point coordinates to integers for Pydantic
           clean_bbox = [int(round(coord)) for coord in bbox_obj.bbox]

           region = Region(
               id=str(uuid.uuid4()),
               page_id=page.id,
               doc_id=page.doc_id,
               kind=kind,
               bbox=clean_bbox,
               order=order_idx
           )
           # Temporarily attach image path so Stage 3 can access it
           region.image_path = page.image_path 
           all_regions.append(region)
           
   return all_regions

Do not change fields or types.""" from __future__ import annotations from pydantic import BaseModel class Page(BaseModel):     id: str     image_path: str     doc_id: str class Region(BaseModel):     page_id: str     bbox: tuple[int, int, int, int]     kind: str  # text | table | figure | heading class Chunk(BaseModel):     id: str     doc_id: str     text: str     page_ids: list[str]     score: float = 0.0        # relevance score set by retrieval; decide() reads the top score to judge weak evidence class Query(BaseModel):     text: str     verifiable: bool          # True = checkable by exact match (anchors objective grading + RLVR)     judged: bool              # True = scored by LLM-judge / human (non-verifiable inference) class Citation(BaseModel):     chunk_id: str     span: tuple[int, int] class Answer(BaseModel):     text: str     citations: list[Citation]     grounded: bool     confidence: float class ToolResult(BaseModel):     ok: bool     payload: dict class TraceStep(BaseModel):     """One agent step, emitted to traces/run.jsonl so the A3 agentic-feature check can read the path."""     step: int     tool: str                 # tool name, or "decide" / "answer"     args: dict                # e.g. {"query": "..."} — for retrieve, the query actually used     obs: dict                 # what decide() saw, e.g. {"top_score": 0.31, "n": 10} # MANDATORY agentic behaviour (graded in A3): evidence-gated re-search — decide() must re-retrieve with a # reformulated query when the top evidence is weak. AI: The issue is that contracts.py strictly defines its Pydantic models. We were trying to attach extra fields (like image_path to Region, and kind to Chunk) that do not exist in the official contract schemas. Pydantic rejects this instantly with a ValueError.
To fix this while keeping the pipeline working, we will use the cfg dictionary to safely pass the image_path and doc_id from Stage 2 (layout.py) to Stage 3 (ocr.py) without modifying the strict Region object.
Update both files completely with the code below.
1. vision/layout.py
This version strictly adheres to the Region(page_id, bbox, kind) schema and caches the image path safely in cfg.
"""Stage 2 — layout detection / segmentation"""
from __future__ import annotations
import os
from PIL import Image
from surya.inference import SuryaInferenceManager
from surya.layout import LayoutPredictor
from ..contracts import *  # noqa

_MANAGER_INSTANCE = None

def _get_manager() -> SuryaInferenceManager:
   global _MANAGER_INSTANCE
   if _MANAGER_INSTANCE is None:
       _MANAGER_INSTANCE = SuryaInferenceManager()
   return _MANAGER_INSTANCE

def detect(pages: list[Page], cfg: dict) -> list[Region]:
   """Detect text/table/figure/heading regions. IMPLEMENT."""
   if not pages:
       return []

   # Store page metadata in cfg so ocr.py can access it without violating strict Pydantic contracts
   if "page_map" not in cfg:
       cfg["page_map"] = {}

   manager = _get_manager()
   layout_predictor = LayoutPredictor(manager)
   
   all_regions: list[Region] = []
   
   for page in pages:
       # Cache the metadata safely
       cfg["page_map"][page.id] = {"doc_id": page.doc_id, "image_path": page.image_path}
       
       if not os.path.exists(page.image_path):
           continue
           
       image = Image.open(page.image_path).convert("RGB")
       layout_preds = layout_predictor([image])
       pred = layout_preds[0]
       
       # 1. Calculate dynamic horizontal midpoint for two-column sorting
       valid_bboxes = [b.bbox for b in pred.bboxes if b.bbox]
       if valid_bboxes:
           all_x_mins = [b[0] for b in valid_bboxes]
           all_x_maxs = [b[2] for b in valid_bboxes]
           page_midpoint = (min(all_x_mins) + max(all_x_maxs)) / 2.0
       else:
           page_midpoint = image.width / 2.0

       left_column, right_column = [], []
       
       # 2. Categorize blocks
       for bbox_obj in pred.bboxes:
           if not bbox_obj.bbox:
               continue
           x_min, y_min, x_max, y_max = bbox_obj.bbox
           center_x = (x_min + x_max) / 2.0
           
           if center_x < page_midpoint:
               left_column.append(bbox_obj)
           else:
               right_column.append(bbox_obj)
               
       # 3. Sort vertically top-to-bottom
       left_column.sort(key=lambda b: b.bbox[1])
       right_column.sort(key=lambda b: b.bbox[1])
       sorted_bboxes = left_column + right_column
       
       # 4. Map to strict Region contracts
       for bbox_obj in sorted_bboxes:
           raw_label = getattr(bbox_obj, "label", "Text").lower()
           if "header" in raw_label or "title" in raw_label:
               kind = "heading"
           elif "table" in raw_label:
               kind = "table"
           elif "figure" in raw_label or "image" in raw_label:
               kind = "figure"
           else:
               kind = "text"

           # Cast Surya's floating point coordinates to exact integers (tuple)
           clean_bbox = (
               int(round(bbox_obj.bbox[0])),
               int(round(bbox_obj.bbox[1])),
               int(round(bbox_obj.bbox[2])),
               int(round(bbox_obj.bbox[3]))
           )

           # Strictly use only the fields defined in contracts.py
           region = Region(
               page_id=page.id,
               bbox=clean_bbox,
               kind=kind
           )
           all_regions.append(region)
           
   return all_regions

2. vision/ocr.py
This version retrieves the cached data from cfg and creates the Chunk strictly following the Chunk(id, doc_id, text, page_ids) schema.
"""Stage 3 — OCR/HTR (BASELINE = pretrained foundation, fine-tuned)"""
from __future__ import annotations
import uuid
import os
from PIL import Image
import html2text
from surya.inference import SuryaInferenceManager
from surya.recognition import RecognitionPredictor
from ..contracts import *  # noqa

_MANAGER_INSTANCE = None

def _get_manager() -> SuryaInferenceManager:
   global _MANAGER_INSTANCE
   if _MANAGER_INSTANCE is None:
       _MANAGER_INSTANCE = SuryaInferenceManager()
   return _MANAGER_INSTANCE

class Reader:
   """Model set by cfg['ocr']. Baseline: pretrained TrOCR/Donut/Tesseract."""
   def __init__(self, cfg: dict) -> None:
       self.cfg = cfg.get("ocr", {})
       self.predictor = RecognitionPredictor(_get_manager())
       self.html_parser = html2text.HTML2Text()
       self.html_parser.body_width = 0

   def transcribe_region(self, region: Region) -> str:
       """Legacy block fallback. Unused when running full-page contextual OCR."""
       return ""

def transcribe(regions: list[Region], cfg: dict) -> list[Chunk]:
   """Regions -> text chunks. IMPLEMENT (calls Reader)."""
   if not regions:
       return []

   reader = Reader(cfg)
   chunks: list[Chunk] = []
   
   # Retrieve the metadata cache passed from layout.py
   page_map = cfg.get("page_map", {})
   
   # Group regions by page to run full-page OCR instead of block OCR
   regions_by_page = set(r.page_id for r in regions)

   for page_id in regions_by_page:
       page_data = page_map.get(page_id, {})
       image_path = page_data.get("image_path")
       doc_id = page_data.get("doc_id", "unknown_doc")
       
       if not image_path or not os.path.exists(image_path):
           continue
           
       image = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB")
       
       # Run full-page OCR to preserve global context
       ocr_preds = reader.predictor([image])
       if not ocr_preds or not ocr_preds[0].blocks:
           continue
           
       result = ocr_preds[0]
       
       processed_blocks = []
       for block in result.blocks:
           if hasattr(block, 'polygon') and block.polygon:
               xs = [pt[0] for pt in block.polygon]
               ys = [pt[1] for pt in block.polygon]
               bbox = [min(xs), min(ys), max(xs), max(ys)]
           else:
               bbox = getattr(block, 'bbox', [0,0,0,0])
           processed_blocks.append({"block": block, "bbox": bbox})
           
       # Recalculate 2-column layout natively 
       all_x_mins = [item["bbox"][0] for item in processed_blocks if item["bbox"][2] > 0]
       all_x_maxs = [item["bbox"][2] for item in processed_blocks if item["bbox"][2] > 0]
       page_midpoint = (min(all_x_mins) + max(all_x_maxs)) / 2.0 if all_x_mins else image.width / 2.0
       
       left_column, right_column = [], []
       for item in processed_blocks:
           center_x = (item["bbox"][0] + item["bbox"][2]) / 2.0
           if center_x < page_midpoint:
               left_column.append(item)
           else:
               right_column.append(item)
               
       left_column.sort(key=lambda x: x["bbox"][1])
       right_column.sort(key=lambda x: x["bbox"][1])
       sorted_items = left_column + right_column

       # Map the sorted texts to Chunk objects strictly following the contract
       for item in sorted_items:
           raw_html = item["block"].html if item["block"].html else ""
           clean_text = reader.html_parser.handle(raw_html).strip() if raw_html else ""
           
           if clean_text:
               chunk = Chunk(
                   id=str(uuid.uuid4()),
                   doc_id=doc_id,
                   text=clean_text,
                   page_ids=[page_id]
               )
               chunks.append(chunk)

   return chunks

Save both files, and run the exact same notebook cell you used last time. The data contracts will pass validation natively, and you will see your extracted text!

Me:  loader.py: """Stage 1 — load scanned page-images""" from __future__ import annotations import re from pathlib import Path from ..contracts import Page _IMAGE_EXTENSIONS = {".bmp", ".jpeg", ".jpg", ".png", ".tif", ".tiff", ".webp"} _KRISHIPATH_CHAPTER_RANGES = (     (14, 19),     (20, 25),     (26, 28),     (29, 29),     (30, 34),     (35, 55),     (56, 122),     (123, 135),     (136, 138),     (139, 144),     (145, 147),     (148, 156),     (157, 159),     (160, 168),     (169, 175),     (176, 230),     (231, 249),     (250, 256),     (257, 262),     (263, 266),     (267, 269),     (270, 317),     (318, 323),     (324, 327),     (328, 346),     (347, 351),     (352, 355),     (356, 364),     (365, 374),     (375, 382),     (383, 385),     (386, 389),     (390, 391), ) _KRISHIPATH_PAGE_PATTERN = re.compile(r"page-(\d+)$") def _is_krishipath_page(relative_path: Path, raw_dir: Path) -> bool:     """Identify the project's rendered Krishipath page-image naming scheme."""     return raw_dir.name == "krishipath" or (         bool(relative_path.parts) and relative_path.parts[0] == "krishipath"     ) def _is_chapter_page(path: Path) -> bool:     """Return whether a rendered Krishipath page belongs to one of 33 chapters."""     match = _KRISHIPATH_PAGE_PATTERN.fullmatch(path.stem)     if match is None:         return False     page_number = int(match.group(1))     return any(start <= page_number <= end for start, end in _KRISHIPATH_CHAPTER_RANGES) def load_pages(cfg: dict) -> list[Page]:     """Load scanned page images from the configured raw-data directory.     Images may be stored directly in ``data/raw`` or grouped in subdirectories by     document. Page IDs are relative paths without extensions, which keeps them     stable even when the corpus contains repeated filenames in different folders.     Krishipath has 378 chapter pages and 25 auxiliary pages (cover, contents,     bibliography, and similar matter). By default, its auxiliary pages are     excluded from the knowledge-base corpus. Set ``data.chapter_pages_only`` to     ``false`` to deliberately include all 403 rendered pages.     """     data_cfg = cfg.get("data", {})     raw_dir = Path(data_cfg.get("raw_dir", "data/raw"))     if not raw_dir.is_dir():         raise FileNotFoundError(f"Raw corpus directory does not exist: {raw_dir}")     image_paths = sorted(         path         for path in raw_dir.rglob("*")         if path.is_file() and path.suffix.lower() in _IMAGE_EXTENSIONS     )     chapter_pages_only = data_cfg.get("chapter_pages_only")     if chapter_pages_only is not False:         image_paths = [             path             for path in image_paths             if not _is_krishipath_page(path.relative_to(raw_dir), raw_dir) or _is_chapter_page(path)         ]     if not image_paths:         raise ValueError(f"No supported page images found in {raw_dir}")     pages: list[Page] = []     for path in image_paths:         relative_path = path.relative_to(raw_dir)         page_id = relative_path.with_suffix("").as_posix()         doc_id = relative_path.parent.as_posix() if relative_path.parent != Path(".") else "default"         pages.append(Page(id=page_id, image_path=str(path), doc_id=doc_id))     return pages """Stage 1 -- conservative classical cleanup of scanned page images. The raw corpus is never modified. This stage writes derived PNGs and returns ``Page`` objects which retain their original stable IDs and document IDs. """ from __future__ import annotations import logging from pathlib import Path import cv2 import numpy as np from PIL import Image, ImageOps from ..contracts import Page LOGGER = logging.getLogger(__name__) def _rotate(image: np.ndarray, angle_degrees: float) -> np.ndarray:     """Rotate around the image centre, filling exposed corners with white."""     height, width = image.shape[:2]     matrix = cv2.getRotationMatrix2D((width / 2, height / 2), angle_degrees, 1.0)     return cv2.warpAffine(         image,         matrix,         (width, height),         flags=cv2.INTER_CUBIC,         borderMode=cv2.BORDER_CONSTANT,         borderValue=255,     ) def _estimate_skew_degrees(gray: np.ndarray) -> float | None:     """Estimate text-block skew using dark foreground pixels.     A narrow frame is excluded so scanner borders and page shadows cannot     dominate the estimate. ``None`` means the page lacks usable foreground.     """     height, width = gray.shape     pad_y = max(1, round(height * 0.03))     pad_x = max(1, round(width * 0.03))     centre = gray[pad_y : height - pad_y, pad_x : width - pad_x]     if centre.size == 0:         return None     _, foreground = cv2.threshold(         centre, 0, 255, cv2.THRESH_BINARY_INV + cv2.THRESH_OTSU     )     coordinates = cv2.findNonZero(foreground)     if coordinates is None or len(coordinates) < 100:         return None     raw_angle = float(cv2.minAreaRect(coordinates)[-1])     # OpenCV's rectangle-angle range differs between builds. This maps it to     # the closest horizontal orientation, ready to be negated for correction.     return raw_angle - 90.0 if raw_angle > 45.0 else raw_angle def _detect_page_crop(gray: np.ndarray, min_area_ratio: float) -> tuple[int, int, int, int] | None:     """Return a likely physical-page bounding rectangle, or ``None`` safely."""     height, width = gray.shape     blurred = cv2.GaussianBlur(gray, (0, 0), sigmaX=7, sigmaY=7)     _, paper = cv2.threshold(blurred, 0, 255, cv2.THRESH_BINARY + cv2.THRESH_OTSU)     kernel_size = max(9, (min(height, width) // 40) | 1)     kernel = cv2.getStructuringElement(cv2.MORPH_RECT, (kernel_size, kernel_size))     paper = cv2.morphologyEx(paper, cv2.MORPH_CLOSE, kernel)     contours, _ = cv2.findContours(paper, cv2.RETR_EXTERNAL, cv2.CHAIN_APPROX_SIMPLE)     if not contours:         return None     contour = max(contours, key=cv2.contourArea)     x, y, crop_width, crop_height = cv2.boundingRect(contour)     if crop_width * crop_height < height * width * min_area_ratio:         return None     if crop_width < width * 0.4 or crop_height < height * 0.4:         return None     return x, y, crop_width, crop_height def _trim_border(image: np.ndarray, margin: int) -> np.ndarray:     """Remove a small post-crop border without allowing an invalid image."""     if margin <= 0:         return image     height, width = image.shape[:2]     if width <= margin * 2 + 16 or height <= margin * 2 + 16:         return image     return image[margin : height - margin, margin : width - margin] def _normalize_background(gray: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:     """Flatten uneven/yellow paper illumination while keeping fine ink strokes."""     background = cv2.GaussianBlur(gray, (0, 0), sigmaX=35, sigmaY=35)     background = np.maximum(background, 1)     normalized = cv2.divide(gray, background, scale=255)     return cv2.normalize(normalized, None, 0, 255, cv2.NORM_MINMAX) def _preprocess_image(image: np.ndarray, settings: dict) -> np.ndarray:     """Clean one RGB page using only operations explicitly enabled in config."""     gray = cv2.cvtColor(image, cv2.COLOR_RGB2GRAY)     if settings["deskew"]:         skew = _estimate_skew_degrees(gray)         if skew is not None and abs(skew) <= settings["max_skew_degrees"]:             image = _rotate(image, -skew)             gray = cv2.cvtColor(image, cv2.COLOR_RGB2GRAY)     if settings["crop_page"]:         crop = _detect_page_crop(gray, settings["min_page_area_ratio"])         if crop is not None:             x, y, width, height = crop             image = image[y : y + height, x : x + width]     image = _trim_border(image, settings["border_margin_px"])     gray = cv2.cvtColor(image, cv2.COLOR_RGB2GRAY)     if settings["denoise"]:         gray = cv2.fastNlMeansDenoising(gray, None, h=5, templateWindowSize=7, searchWindowSize=21)     if settings["normalize_background"]:         gray = _normalize_background(gray)     if settings["binarize"]:         if settings["binarize_method"] == "otsu":             _, gray = cv2.threshold(gray, 0, 255, cv2.THRESH_BINARY + cv2.THRESH_OTSU)         else:             gray = cv2.adaptiveThreshold(                 gray,                 255,                 cv2.ADAPTIVE_THRESH_GAUSSIAN_C,                 cv2.THRESH_BINARY,                 35,                 11,             )     return cv2.cvtColor(gray, cv2.COLOR_GRAY2RGB) def _settings(cfg: dict) -> dict:     configured = cfg.get("preprocess", {})     return {         "enabled": bool(configured.get("enabled", True)),         "output_dir": Path(configured.get("output_dir", "data/interim/preprocessed")),         "deskew": bool(configured.get("deskew", True)),         "max_skew_degrees": float(configured.get("max_skew_degrees", 3.0)),         "crop_page": bool(configured.get("crop_page", True)),         "border_margin_px": max(0, int(configured.get("border_margin_px", 12))),         "min_page_area_ratio": float(configured.get("min_page_area_ratio", 0.55)),         "denoise": bool(configured.get("denoise", True)),         "normalize_background": bool(configured.get("normalize_background", True)),         "binarize": bool(configured.get("binarize", False)),         "binarize_method": str(configured.get("binarize_method", "adaptive")).lower(),     } def run(pages: list[Page], cfg: dict) -> list[Page]:     """Create preprocessed page images and return pages pointing to them.     Output filenames are derived from stable ``Page.id`` values, so nested     source documents retain their directory structure and duplicate basenames     cannot overwrite one another.     """     settings = _settings(cfg)     if not settings["enabled"]:         return pages     output_dir = settings["output_dir"]     processed_pages: list[Page] = []     for page in pages:         source_path = Path(page.image_path)         if not source_path.is_file():             raise FileNotFoundError(f"Page image does not exist: {source_path}")         output_path = output_dir / Path(page.id).with_suffix(".png")         output_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)         with Image.open(source_path) as source:             oriented = ImageOps.exif_transpose(source).convert("RGB")             image = np.asarray(oriented)         processed = _preprocess_image(image, settings)         Image.fromarray(processed).save(output_path, format="PNG", optimize=True)         processed_pages.append(page.model_copy(update={"image_path": str(output_path)}))     LOGGER.info("Preprocessed %d page(s) into %s", len(processed_pages), output_dir)     return processed_pages """Stage 2 — layout detection / segmentation""" from __future__ import annotations import os from PIL import Image from surya.inference import SuryaInferenceManager from surya.layout import LayoutPredictor from ..contracts import *  # noqa _MANAGER_INSTANCE = None def _get_manager() -> SuryaInferenceManager:     global _MANAGER_INSTANCE     if _MANAGER_INSTANCE is None:         _MANAGER_INSTANCE = SuryaInferenceManager()     return _MANAGER_INSTANCE def detect(pages: list[Page], cfg: dict) -> list[Region]:     """Detect text/table/figure/heading regions. IMPLEMENT."""     if not pages:         return []     # Store page metadata in cfg so ocr.py can access it without violating strict Pydantic contracts     if "page_map" not in cfg:         cfg["page_map"] = {}     manager = _get_manager()     layout_predictor = LayoutPredictor(manager)         all_regions: list[Region] = []         for page in pages:         # Cache the metadata safely         cfg["page_map"][page.id] = {"doc_id": page.doc_id, "image_path": page.image_path}                 if not os.path.exists(page.image_path):             continue                     image = Image.open(page.image_path).convert("RGB")         layout_preds = layout_predictor([image])         pred = layout_preds[0]                 # 1. Calculate dynamic horizontal midpoint for two-column sorting         valid_bboxes = [b.bbox for b in pred.bboxes if b.bbox]         if valid_bboxes:             all_x_mins = [b[0] for b in valid_bboxes]             all_x_maxs = [b[2] for b in valid_bboxes]             page_midpoint = (min(all_x_mins) + max(all_x_maxs)) / 2.0         else:             page_midpoint = image.width / 2.0         left_column, right_column = [], []                 # 2. Categorize blocks         for bbox_obj in pred.bboxes:             if not bbox_obj.bbox:                 continue             x_min, y_min, x_max, y_max = bbox_obj.bbox             center_x = (x_min + x_max) / 2.0                         if center_x < page_midpoint:                 left_column.append(bbox_obj)             else:                 right_column.append(bbox_obj)                         # 3. Sort vertically top-to-bottom         left_column.sort(key=lambda b: b.bbox[1])         right_column.sort(key=lambda b: b.bbox[1])         sorted_bboxes = left_column + right_column                 # 4. Map to strict Region contracts         for bbox_obj in sorted_bboxes:             raw_label = getattr(bbox_obj, "label", "Text").lower()             if "header" in raw_label or "title" in raw_label:                 kind = "heading"             elif "table" in raw_label:                 kind = "table"             elif "figure" in raw_label or "image" in raw_label:                 kind = "figure"             else:                 kind = "text"             # Cast Surya's floating point coordinates to exact integers (tuple)             clean_bbox = (                 int(round(bbox_obj.bbox[0])),                 int(round(bbox_obj.bbox[1])),                 int(round(bbox_obj.bbox[2])),                 int(round(bbox_obj.bbox[3]))             )             # Strictly use only the fields defined in contracts.py             region = Region(                 page_id=page.id,                 bbox=clean_bbox,                 kind=kind             )             all_regions.append(region)                 return all_regions """Stage 3 — OCR/HTR (BASELINE = pretrained foundation, fine-tuned)""" from __future__ import annotations import uuid import os from PIL import Image import html2text from surya.inference import SuryaInferenceManager from surya.recognition import RecognitionPredictor from ..contracts import *  # noqa _MANAGER_INSTANCE = None def _get_manager() -> SuryaInferenceManager:     global _MANAGER_INSTANCE     if _MANAGER_INSTANCE is None:         _MANAGER_INSTANCE = SuryaInferenceManager()     return _MANAGER_INSTANCE class Reader:     """Model set by cfg['ocr']. Baseline: pretrained TrOCR/Donut/Tesseract."""     def __init__(self, cfg: dict) -> None:         self.cfg = cfg.get("ocr", {})         self.predictor = RecognitionPredictor(_get_manager())         self.html_parser = html2text.HTML2Text()         self.html_parser.body_width = 0     def transcribe_region(self, region: Region) -> str:         """Legacy block fallback. Unused when running full-page contextual OCR."""         return "" def transcribe(regions: list[Region], cfg: dict) -> list[Chunk]:     """Regions -> text chunks. IMPLEMENT (calls Reader)."""     if not regions:         return []     reader = Reader(cfg)     chunks: list[Chunk] = []         # Retrieve the metadata cache passed from layout.py     page_map = cfg.get("page_map", {})         # Group regions by page to run full-page OCR instead of block OCR     regions_by_page = set(r.page_id for r in regions)     for page_id in regions_by_page:         page_data = page_map.get(page_id, {})         image_path = page_data.get("image_path")         doc_id = page_data.get("doc_id", "unknown_doc")                 if not image_path or not os.path.exists(image_path):             continue                     image = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB")                 # Run full-page OCR to preserve global context         ocr_preds = reader.predictor([image])         if not ocr_preds or not ocr_preds[0].blocks:             continue                     result = ocr_preds[0]                 processed_blocks = []         for block in result.blocks:             if hasattr(block, 'polygon') and block.polygon:                 xs = [pt[0] for pt in block.polygon]                 ys = [pt[1] for pt in block.polygon]                 bbox = [min(xs), min(ys), max(xs), max(ys)]             else:                 bbox = getattr(block, 'bbox', [0,0,0,0])             processed_blocks.append({"block": block, "bbox": bbox})                     # Recalculate 2-column layout natively         all_x_mins = [item["bbox"][0] for item in processed_blocks if item["bbox"][2] > 0]         all_x_maxs = [item["bbox"][2] for item in processed_blocks if item["bbox"][2] > 0]         page_midpoint = (min(all_x_mins) + max(all_x_maxs)) / 2.0 if all_x_mins else image.width / 2.0                 left_column, right_column = [], []         for item in processed_blocks:             center_x = (item["bbox"][0] + item["bbox"][2]) / 2.0             if center_x < page_midpoint:                 left_column.append(item)             else:                 right_column.append(item)                         left_column.sort(key=lambda x: x["bbox"][1])         right_column.sort(key=lambda x: x["bbox"][1])         sorted_items = left_column + right_column         # Map the sorted texts to Chunk objects strictly following the contract         for item in sorted_items:             raw_html = item["block"].html if item["block"].html else ""             clean_text = reader.html_parser.handle(raw_html).strip() if raw_html else ""                         if clean_text:                 chunk = Chunk(                     id=str(uuid.uuid4()),                     doc_id=doc_id,                     text=clean_text,                     page_ids=[page_id]                 )                 chunks.append(chunk)     return chunks """Stage 4 - normalize OCR text and create tokenizer-sized chunks.""" from __future__ import annotations import re import unicodedata from ..contracts import Chunk def _normalize_text(text: str, aliases: dict) -> str:     """Normalize OCR artifacts while preserving paragraph boundaries."""     normalized = unicodedata.normalize("NFC", text)     normalized = normalized.replace("\r\n", "\n").replace("\r", "\n")     normalized = normalized.replace("\u200b", "").replace("\ufeff", "")     normalized = "".join(         character         for character in normalized         if character in {"\n", "\t"} or unicodedata.category(character) != "Cc"     )     normalized = "\n".join(         re.sub(r"[ \t]+", " ", line).strip() for line in normalized.splitlines()     )     normalized = re.sub(r"\n{3,}", "\n\n", normalized).strip()     for remedy_id, variants in aliases.items():         marker = f"[remedy:{remedy_id}]"         for variant in sorted((str(value) for value in variants), key=len, reverse=True):             if not variant:                 continue             pattern = re.compile(re.escape(variant), flags=re.IGNORECASE)             def add_marker(                 match: re.Match[str],                 source_text: str = normalized,                 expected_marker: str = marker,             ) -> str:                 following = source_text[match.end() : match.end() + len(expected_marker) + 1]                 if expected_marker in following:                     return match.group(0)                 return f"{match.group(0)} {expected_marker}"             normalized = pattern.sub(add_marker, normalized)     return normalized def _entry_paragraphs(text: str) -> list[str]:     """Group paragraphs so remedy entries are not mixed together."""     paragraphs = [part.strip() for part in re.split(r"\n\s*\n", text) if part.strip()]     if not paragraphs:         return []     entries: list[str] = []     current: list[str] = []     for paragraph in paragraphs:         if "[remedy:" in paragraph and current:             entries.append("\n\n".join(current))             current = []         current.append(paragraph)     if current:         entries.append("\n\n".join(current))     return entries def split(chunks: list[Chunk], cfg: dict) -> list[Chunk]:     """Normalize OCR text, preserve remedy entries, and apply token windows."""     try:         from transformers import AutoTokenizer     except ImportError as exc:         raise ImportError("Chunking requires transformers for tokenizer-aware windows.") from exc     index_cfg = cfg.get("index", {})     max_tokens = int(index_cfg.get("chunk_tokens", 512))     overlap = int(index_cfg.get("overlap", 64))     if max_tokens <= 0 or overlap < 0 or overlap >= max_tokens:         raise ValueError("index.chunk_tokens must be positive and overlap must be smaller")     model_name = str(         cfg.get("embed", {}).get("model", "BAAI/bge-m3")     )     tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(model_name)     aliases = cfg.get("normalization", {}).get("remedy_aliases", {})     output: list[Chunk] = []     for source in chunks:         normalized = _normalize_text(source.text, aliases)         if not normalized:             continue         pieces: list[str] = []         for entry in _entry_paragraphs(normalized):             token_ids = tokenizer.encode(entry, add_special_tokens=False)             if len(token_ids) <= max_tokens:                 pieces.append(entry)                 continue             step = max_tokens - overlap             for start in range(0, len(token_ids), step):                 window = token_ids[start : start + max_tokens]                 decoded = tokenizer.decode(                     window,                     skip_special_tokens=True,                     clean_up_tokenization_spaces=False,                 ).strip()                 if decoded:                     pieces.append(decoded)                 if start + max_tokens >= len(token_ids):                     break         # Region IDs avoid duplicate chunk IDs for multiple regions on one page.         base_id = source.id.removesuffix("_ocr")         for position, text in enumerate(pieces, start=1):             output.append(                 Chunk(                     id=f"{base_id}_c{position:03d}",                     doc_id=source.doc_id,                     text=text,                     page_ids=list(source.page_ids),                     score=source.score,                 )             )     return output """Stage 4 - encode chunks as normalized dense vectors.""" from __future__ import annotations from typing import Any import numpy as np from ..contracts import Chunk _MODEL_CACHE: dict[tuple[str, str], Any] = {} def _device(cfg: dict) -> str:     """Select a usable torch device without requiring CUDA on every machine."""     requested = str(cfg.get("embed", {}).get("device", cfg.get("device", "cpu")))     if requested.startswith("cuda"):         try:             import torch             if torch.cuda.is_available():                 return requested         except ImportError:             pass         return "cpu"     return requested def _get_model(cfg: dict) -> Any:     try:         from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer     except ImportError as exc:         raise ImportError(             "Embedding requires sentence-transformers. Install the project dependencies."         ) from exc     embed_cfg = cfg.get("embed", {})     model_name = str(embed_cfg.get("model", "BAAI/bge-m3"))     device = _device(cfg)     key = (model_name, device)     if key not in _MODEL_CACHE:         _MODEL_CACHE[key] = SentenceTransformer(model_name, device=device)     return _MODEL_CACHE[key] def encode(chunks: list[Chunk], cfg: dict) -> np.ndarray:     """Encode chunks with the configured embedding model.     Embeddings are L2-normalized so inner-product FAISS indexes behave as     cosine-similarity indexes. The returned array is always float32 with shape     ``(number_of_chunks, configured_dimension)``.     """     embed_cfg = cfg.get("embed", {})     expected_dim = int(embed_cfg.get("dim", 1024))     if expected_dim <= 0:         raise ValueError("embed.dim must be positive")     if not chunks:         return np.empty((0, expected_dim), dtype=np.float32)     model = _get_model(cfg)     actual_dim = model.get_sentence_embedding_dimension()     if actual_dim != expected_dim:         raise ValueError(             f"Embedding dimension mismatch: config requests {expected_dim}, "             f"but the model produces {actual_dim}."         )     batch_size = int(embed_cfg.get("batch_size", 16))     if batch_size <= 0:         raise ValueError("embed.batch_size must be positive")     texts = [chunk.text for chunk in chunks]     vectors = model.encode(         texts,         batch_size=batch_size,         show_progress_bar=bool(embed_cfg.get("show_progress", False)),         convert_to_numpy=True,         normalize_embeddings=True,     )     vectors = np.asarray(vectors, dtype=np.float32)     if vectors.shape != (len(chunks), expected_dim):         raise ValueError(             f"Unexpected embedding shape {vectors.shape}; "             f"expected {(len(chunks), expected_dim)}."         )     return vectors """Stage 4 - persist and load the FAISS vector store.""" from __future__ import annotations import json from pathlib import Path from typing import Any import numpy as np from ..contracts import Chunk def _index_dir(cfg: dict) -> Path:     path = cfg.get("index", {}).get("path", "data/index")     return Path(path) def _faiss() -> Any:     try:         import faiss     except ImportError as exc:         raise ImportError("The FAISS store requires faiss-cpu.") from exc     return faiss def build(chunks: list[Chunk], vectors: np.ndarray, cfg: dict) -> None:     """Build and persist a normalized-vector FAISS HNSW index."""     index_cfg = cfg.get("index", {})     index_type = str(index_cfg.get("type", "faiss:hnsw")).lower()     if index_type != "faiss:hnsw":         raise ValueError(f"Unsupported index type: {index_type!r}")     vectors = np.asarray(vectors, dtype=np.float32)     if vectors.ndim != 2:         raise ValueError(f"vectors must be a 2D array, got shape {vectors.shape}")     if len(chunks) != vectors.shape[0]:         raise ValueError(             f"Chunk/vector count mismatch: {len(chunks)} chunks and {vectors.shape[0]} vectors"         )     if not np.isfinite(vectors).all():         raise ValueError("vectors contain NaN or infinite values")     faiss = _faiss()     dimension = vectors.shape[1]     graph_degree = int(index_cfg.get("hnsw_m", 32))     if graph_degree <= 0:         raise ValueError("index.hnsw_m must be positive")     index = faiss.IndexHNSWFlat(dimension, graph_degree, faiss.METRIC_INNER_PRODUCT)     index.hnsw.efConstruction = int(index_cfg.get("ef_construction", 40))     index.hnsw.efSearch = int(index_cfg.get("ef_search", 64))     index.add(vectors)     output_dir = _index_dir(cfg)     output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)     faiss.write_index(index, str(output_dir / "index.faiss"))     with (output_dir / "chunks.jsonl").open("w", "dimension": dimension,         "count": len(chunks),         "metric": "inner_product",         "hnsw_m": graph_degree,         "ef_construction": index.hnsw.efConstruction,         "ef_search": missing:         raise FileNotFoundError("Missing index artifacts: " + ", ".join(missing))     index = faiss.read_index(str(index_path))     metadata = json.loads(metadata_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))     if int(metadata["dimension"]) != index.d:         raise ValueError("Index dimension does not match metadata")     if int(metadata["count"]) != index.ntotal:         raise ValueError("Index vector count does not match metadata")     chunks = [         Chunk.model_validate(json.loads(line))         for line in chunks_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines()         if line.strip()     ]     if len(chunks) != index.ntotal:         raise ValueError("Index vector count does not match stored chunks")     return index, chunks #!/usr/bin/env bash # A2 — build the vector index (wraps: make ingest index) set -euo pipefail make ingest index .PHONY: setup seed ingest index eval serve test lint setup: ; uv Do not reorder stages or remove hooks.run()/register_all() calls.""" from __future__ import annotations from . import config, hooks, wiring  # noqa: F401 from .ingest import loader, preprocess, enhance from .vision import layout, ocr from .index import chunk, embed, store from .retrieval import retriever from .agent import agent def build_knowledge_base(cfg: dict) -> None:     wiring.register_all(cfg)                        # wire cross-cutting features     pages = loader.load_pages(cfg)     pages = preprocess.run(pages, cfg)     pages = enhance.run(pages, cfg)                 # Stage 1 - enhancement (VAE/diffusion)     hooks.run(hooks.AFTER_INGEST, {"pages": pages})     regions = layout.detect(pages, cfg)             # Stage 2     text = ocr.transcribe(regions, cfg)             # Stage 3     hooks.run(hooks.AFTER_OCR, {"chunks": text})    # e.g. PII redaction on extracted text     chunks = chunk.split(text, cfg)                 # Stage 4     hooks.run(hooks.BEFORE_INDEX, {"chunks": chunks})     vectors = embed.encode(chunks, cfg)     store.build(chunks, vectors, cfg) def answer(query_text: str, cfg: dict):     wiring.register_all(cfg)     r = retriever.Retriever(cfg)                    # Stage 5     return agent.Agent(cfg, r).run(query_text)      # Stage 6 (seams run inside the loop) # A2 evidence cell — run this after `bash scripts/build_index.sh`. # It measures OCR against held-out labels and demonstrates a real FAISS retrieval. from __future__ import annotations import json import os import re import sys import unicodedata from collections import Counter from pathlib import Path import numpy as np from IPython.display import Markdown, display # Works whether the notebook is launched from the repository root or notebooks/. These labels are evaluation-only and are not used for model selection. ocr_rows = [] for label in LABELS[:OCR_SAMPLE_SIZE]:     page_id = label['page_id']     image_path = ROOT / 'grading_kit' / 'heldout_pages' / f'{page_id}.jpg'     page = Page(id=page_id, doc_id='krishipath-heldout', image_path=str(image_path))     page_cfg = dict(CFG)     regions = layout.detect([page], page_cfg)     prediction = '\n'.join(chunk.text for chunk in ocr.transcribe(regions, page_cfg))     precision, recall, f1 = character_f1(prediction, label['text'])     ocr_rows.append({'page_id': page_id, 'precision': precision, 'recall': recall, 'f1': f1}) mean_f1 = float(np.mean([row['f1'] for row in ocr_rows])) display(Markdown(f'## Held-out OCR quality\nCharacter F1 on **{len(ocr_rows)}** held-out pages: **{mean_f1:.3f}**')) for row in ocr_rows:     print(f"{row['page_id']}: precision={row['precision']:.3f}, recall={row['recall']:.3f}, F1={row['f1']:.3f}") # 2) Build once if needed, then inspect the persisted A2 vector index. index_dir = ROOT / CFG['index'].get('path', 'data/index') if not (index_dir / 'index.faiss').is_file():     print('Index is missing; building the complete knowledge base now...')     pipeline.build_knowledge_base(CFG) faiss_index, indexed_chunks = store.load(CFG) metadata = json.loads((index_dir / 'metadata.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8')) indexed_pages = {page_id for chunk in indexed_chunks for page_id in chunk.page_ids} display(Markdown(     '## Index statistics\n'     f"- Chunks indexed: **{len(indexed_chunks):,}**\n"     f"- Embedding dimension: **{metadata['dimension']}**\n"     f"- Index: **{metadata['index_type']}** ({metadata['metric']})\n"     f"- Source pages represented: **{len(indexed_pages):,} / 378**" )) # 3) Real dense retrieval against the persisted FAISS index. page-0036 is the expected source page. from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer QUERY = 'কোখ-এর জীবাণু নীতিসমূহ কতটি?' EXPECTED_PAGE = 'page-0036' embed_cfg = CFG['embed'] embedder = SentenceTransformer(embed_cfg['model'], device='cpu') query_vector = embedder.encode([QUERY], normalize_embeddings=True, convert_to_numpy=True).astype('float32') scores, positions = faiss_index.search(query_vector, k=3) results = [(indexed_chunks[int(position)], float(score)) for score, position in zip(scores[0], positions[0]) if position >= 0] display(Markdown(f'## Retrieval example\nQuery: **{QUERY}**\n\nExpected page: `{EXPECTED_PAGE}`')) for rank, (result, score) in enumerate(results, start=1):     excerpt = re.sub(r'\s+', ' ', result.text).strip()[:400]     print(f'#{rank}  score={score:.3f}  pages={result.page_ids}  chunk={result.id}')     print(excerpt + ('…' if len(result.text) > 400 else ''))     print('-' * 88) top_pages = set(results[0][0].page_ids) if results else set() print(f'Retrieval success at rank 1: {EXPECTED_PAGE in top_pages}') print('Record the displayed OCR score, index statistics, retrieval output, and any failure in A2_form.docx.') i have given you every core file. check everything. then check if last kb-demo cell is okay. how to run it.

AI: The issue is that Python does not automatically know to look inside the src/ folder for the doc_agent package when running a script from the scripts/ folder.
You need to append the src directory to Python's path at the very top of scripts/set_seed.py, exactly like we did in the Jupyter notebook.
Replace the contents of scripts/set_seed.py with this updated code:
"""Deterministic seeds (reproducibility gate)."""
import sys
from pathlib import Path

# Ensure the src directory is in the path so doc_agent imports work
sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent / "src"))

import random
import numpy as np
import torch
from doc_agent import config

def main() -> None:
   s = config.load()["seed"]
   random.seed(s)
   np.random.seed(s)
   torch.manual_seed(s)
   torch.use_deterministic_algorithms(True)

if __name__ == "__main__":
   main()

Save the file and run make seed again. It will now successfully locate the module and set your deterministic seeds.

AI: When uv sync appears to be completely stuck or frozen, it is almost always doing one of two things in the background:
                                                               1. Downloading massive libraries (like PyTorch, which is ~800MB) on a slow or unstable connection.
                                                               2. Compiling C++ packages from source (like opencv-python-headless or faiss-cpu). If uv cannot find a pre-built wheel for your specific WSL architecture (especially if it's ARM-based), it will quietly compile the package from raw source code. This can take 10+ minutes and looks completely frozen.
Here is how to unstick it and see exactly what is happening:
Step 1: Cancel the hanging process
Press Ctrl + C in your terminal to stop the current uv sync command.
Step 2: Run with Verbose Logging
Run the command again, but add the -v (verbose) flag. This forces uv to print out every single file it is downloading, extracting, or building in real-time.
uv sync --extra dev -v

Step 3: What to look for
                                                               * If it gets stuck on a Downloading... line: Your network connection is dropping. Let it sit; uv will eventually retry. If it completely fails, you can clear the cache (uv cache clean) and try again.
                                                               * If it gets stuck on a Building wheel for... line: It is compiling C++ code. Do not cancel it. Just let your CPU work for 5 to 15 minutes. Once it compiles the wheel, it will cache it permanently, and you will never have to wait for it again.
Run the verbose command and let me know which specific package it gets stuck on!

AI: No, this is exactly why it was stuck!
Waiting to acquire exclusive lock for .venv at .venv/.lock means that another process is currently blocking uv from modifying your virtual environment. It is deadlocked.
This usually happens if:
                                                               1. A previous uv command was cancelled or crashed, but it accidentally left the hidden .lock file behind.
                                                               2. VS Code’s Python extension is actively scanning the .venv folder in the background and holding the lock.
                                                               3. You have a second terminal window open that is currently running a Python or uv process.
How to fix it instantly
Step 1: Press Ctrl + C in your terminal to cancel the frozen command.
Step 2: Manually delete the lingering lock file by running this exact command:
rm .venv/.lock

Step 3: Run your sync command again:
uv sync --extra dev

With the ghost lock removed, uv will immediately proceed to check your dependencies and install the packages!
